**Red for the right reason, items `0042` and `0105`.** At `d9d7faa` every test above failed on the missing behaviour - the absent `schemaVersion` read, the unselected version set, the unfiltered vocabulary - with no compile error and no missing fixture; at `7e99515` the same tests pass, unchanged. At `64dbb4e` item `0105`'s unit tests for B-014 failed on the `NotImplementedException` of the member each calls, `SpechtRunner.Evaluate` or `SpecSchemas.Version`, declared with that body by the test commit as item `0034`'s was (`0001-F1` § 8); at `4f1f704`, where the two members are built, the same tests pass, unchanged. The on-disk load's rejection of a pin the tool does not ship (§ 7) is pinned there too, by `SpecSchemasUnitTests`; B-003's row is unchanged, since the check does not take that load.

**No red on record, item `0120`.** This item's tests and its production code landed together in one commit, `6f64a28`. No commit holds the tests without the code, so history records no red state of this item. The tests in that commit are written against `SemanticVersion`, against `SchemaVersion.Number`, `SpecStructure.SchemaVersion` and `SpechtReport.SchemaVersion` as `SemanticVersion`, against `SchemaVersions.Select(SemanticVersion)`, and against the document's `schemaVersion` as a string. The same commit carries the bindings for the steps decision 0005 reworded to `major.minor.patch` in the eight scenarios above, and for the `engine.feature` Background spec-author reworded in `aaf2f5e`; `reqnroll.json` reports a scenario with an unbound step as Skipped, not failed.

**The upstream schema source, item `0176`.** The mechanism of B-068 to B-076 is one read in `SpecManifest.Load`, so every unit test is a manifest built in memory, given a `spechtSchema` with one fault or none, and loaded through a `MockFileSystem`; the source is synthetic, at `https://schemas.example/`, and nothing is fetched. A rejection is asserted as a `SpechtManifestException` whose message holds the member or the kind in quotes, the way the loader's other faults name a key, and not as § 7's whole sentence. The six bound scenarios write the same record into a synthetic root's manifest and launch the built tool: "the manifest is rejected" is exit `3`, and "the rejection names ..." reads stderr for `'location'`, `'version'`, `'hashes'`, `'task'` or `'epic'`. "A folder on the machine" is written as a relative folder, since no fixture carries an absolute path. Three scenarios are not this item's to turn green, and each row is `Partial` on its unit test until then: B-068's last step reads the report document and B-075's needs the check to read the local copy, both item `0092`'s, and B-074's two run `upgrade`, item `0093`'s. B-075's first step is the one B-076 shares, so it is bound, and its other steps are not.

**Red, item `0176`.** The tests name `SpecStructure.SpechtSchema`, `UpstreamSchemaSource.Location`, `Version` and `Files`, and `UpstreamSchemaFile.Kind`, `Address` and `Hash`. Until `implementer` declares them `test/tests` does not compile, on `CS1061` for `SpechtSchema` alone, so the tests by themselves are red on a compile error and not on behaviour: this role adds no production member. With the property standing in as null, outside `src/` and not committed, the two classes ran 151 cases and 22 failed, each on its assertion: the record, the three addresses and the two rows of fetched kinds on a null `SpechtSchema`, and the sixteen rejection cases on a message that reads "the engine does not know the key 'spechtSchema'" and names no member and no kind. The thirteen cases of the six bound scenarios fail on the same message in stderr. Two things are green before the code exists and prove nothing until it does: `AManifestWithNoSpechtSchema_WhenLoaded_ShouldRecordNoUpstreamSchemaSource`, and the five new rows of the input-failure theory, because a manifest carrying a key the engine does not know already exits `3` with an empty stdout.

Hard to test, item `0176`, reported to `implementer`: the command tester sees stdout and the exit code only, so an unknown `spechtSchema` and a rejected one are the same result through the command, and the integration rows and "the manifest is rejected" cannot tell them apart; only the message can, at the unit tier and in stderr. Whether the manifest declares the epic grammar is read from the manifest's text inside the load, so it is reached only through `SpecManifest.Load`, not on its own.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0176`: the order of `Files`, and that no fault repeats the value written, which § 7 states and no claim does; a `spechtSchema` or a `hashes` that is not a JSON object, and a JSON `null` in the key's place (§ 7); a member other than the three and a hash for a kind that is not fetched (OQ-46, open); several faults reported in one rejection, and an absent member raising only its own fault; a `location` followed by a file name the default manifest fills in, where the manifest names none.

**The check under a recorded upstream schema source, item `0092`.** Three mechanisms sit beneath B-024, B-028, B-032 and B-077 to B-081, and each has its own unit class. The read of one file is `SpecManifestStore` over bytes in a `MockFileSystem`. The comparison is `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify` over bytes the test builds and a source built with `UpstreamSchemaSourceFixture` and `UpstreamSchemaFileFixture`, beside the tests in `test/tests/Manifest/`; a recorded hash is always the SHA-256 of bytes the test holds, never a digest written out. The selection is `SpecSchemasLoader` over a manifest and schema files in a `MockFileSystem`: the pinned version is a `SchemaVersionFixture`, whose schemas accept anything, and the local copy is a schema that accepts one constant, so one evaluation says which text a schema was built from. A rejection is asserted as a `SpechtManifestException` whose message holds the file's path under the root with `/` separators and, for an absent file, the text `specht init` - not the whole sentence, so OQ-57 can go either way. B-025 has no mechanism to unit-test (the table names the members read); its proof is the two the owner ruled on 2026-10-12 (OQ-39): `EngineAssembly.NetworkReferencesIn` over the engine's library and the tool's own assembly, and a launch with the network taken away.

The eleven scenarios launch the built tool. Each Given that records a source writes a `spechtSchema` holding the SHA-256 of each frontmatter schema file the root holds. "The root holds the copy of that source under its schema folder" makes the Feature schema of that copy require a key the embedded one does not, records that copy's hashes and writes a Feature without the key, so "the frontmatter is validated against the local copy" is a frontmatter violation on that Feature in the document. "The item schema" is the `task` kind: absent is its file deleted, and edited is one byte appended after its hash was recorded. "The manifest declares no epic grammar" takes `identifiers.epic` out of the root's manifest. "Exactly version 0.1.0's rule ids are evaluated" reads the document's `rulesEvaluated` against the size of that version's vocabulary, the only thing the document says about it; that the ids are that version's is the loader's test and B-014's. "The network is unavailable" points the launched process's six proxy variables at a closed loopback port, through an overload of `AcceptanceTool.Launch` that takes an environment; "the check completes" is exit `0` or `1` with the document on stdout; "no network call was attempted" is the same read of the two built assemblies as the integration test. "The standard error names the item schema's file" looks for `.specht/schema/frontmatter/task.schema.json` as written, and "the command that fetches it" for `specht init`.

**Green after implementation, item `0092`.** The full build passes after integrating `0095`, including the schema store, byte verifier, loader, report mapping and four network-boundary cases. The existing scenarios for B-024, B-025, B-028, B-032 and B-077 to B-081 are bound and pass; B-068 and B-075 now pass over the implemented copy.

**Red record before implementation, item `0092`.** The test pass added no production member. `test/acceptance` compiles: of the 127 cases of `versioning.feature`, 35 ran and 92 are Skipped, and eight of the 35 fail, each on its assertion - B-024 on a document with no frontmatter violation, B-032 and B-068 on a document that says `embedded`, B-077 and B-080 on exit `0`, and B-078, B-079 and B-081 on an empty stderr. Three of this item's scenarios are green before the code exists and prove nothing until it does: B-025, since nothing reaches the network today; B-028, since the embedded version's vocabulary is evaluated either way; and B-075, since the check completes without reading any copy. `test/tests` does not compile: seven files name `SpecManifestStore`, `UpstreamSchemaCopy`, a `SpecSchemasLoader` constructor taking the file system and the store, `SpecSchemas.Source` and `SpechtReport.SchemaSource`, and the only errors are `CS0246`, `CS1729`, `CS1061` and `CS0117` for those five. Before those files were written the tests that need no new member ran: the two new rows of the input-failure theory failed on a stdout that carries the summary, the `--json` test of B-032 failed, and the three cases of `CheckCommandNetworkIntegrationTests` passed, as they do from the start.

Hard to test, item `0092`, reported to `implementer`: a call that is not made leaves nothing to observe, so the launch under dead proxies shows only that the check does not need the network, and the guard checks the two assemblies without reading the libraries beside them, and nothing pins that the schema library is never asked to resolve a reference a fetched schema makes to another address. After integrating item `0095`, the guard permits only `UpstreamSchemaFetcher.Fetch`'s `HttpClient.GetByteArrayAsync` and the host's client constructors and credential-free configuration setters. The known network type references are permitted, and another call to the same fetch API is detected by `AFetchOutsideTheApprovedAdapter_WhenItsReferencesAreRead_ShouldRemainAForbiddenNetworkReference`; the same step is shared with the upgrade scenarios of B-033 and B-067. `UpstreamSchemaCopy.Verify` is handed no root, so that a message carries none is asserted at the loader, over an absolute root the mock file system gives. `SpechtReport.SchemaSource` is an init property, which the generated `SpechtReportFixture` does not set, so a test sets it with a `with` expression. The command tester still sees no stderr, so B-078, B-079 and B-081 reach the process only by launching it.

Unclaimed, so untested, item `0092`: the whole sentence of either rejection, and whether the one for a file that does not match names the kind, a hash or a command; whether `--schemas` is named (OQ-57, open); the order of several faults, which B-078 and B-081 are read as naming one by one; a fault of the manifest beside a fault of the copy; a local file that matches its hash and is not a JSON Schema, or carries a byte-order mark; what the document says of the source's address and version (`0001-F3` OQ-6, open). Selected on-disk provenance (B-011) is now proved by item `0043`'s tests above. B-075 says which kinds are fetched and no claim says which schema the check uses for a kind that is not: the loader's test pins the pinned version's, as the approved plan has it, and the scenario leaves the root's epic schema in place - a gap for `spec-author`.

Hard to test, reported to `implementer`: the check writes stderr through `Console.Error`, not the injected console, so B-003's and B-039's stderr is seen only by launching the tool. B-002, B-003 and B-039 launch the tool over the real embedded set, which holds `0.1.0` alone; B-003's Given asserts that, and fails the day a second version ships. The launched tool cannot be given a version set, so B-055's exactness is proved at the engine and in process, never through the tool's stdout. The frontmatter rule also reports a root-level violation with no identifier beside the keyed one, so a test asserts the keyed violation is present rather than the exact list. The engine gives no member saying which `v<n>` folder a `SchemaVersion` was read from, and from this item `Number` is no longer that integer: the slot test here and the rule page tests (`0001-F3` § 8) find the folder by schema text, through `test/tests/Shared/EmbeddedFolder.cs`, until a member or item `0126`'s rename replaces it.

Unclaimed, so untested: whether a prerelease or build suffix is a version (OQ-15); whether a part with a leading zero (`0.01.0`) is a version (OQ-28, open); a part with a sign, surrounding whitespace, or a number too large for an integer; and what the report names for a pin under a pin policy (B-053, B-054, item `0124`).

`VersioningSteps.Lock` executes B-092 and both B-093 scenarios over synthetic files on disk, calling the same method as the build target. The dedicated `test/build.tests` project references the build assembly and is included in the solution's tier targets. B-092's numeric newest selection and preservation of previous entries are pinned by `SchemaVersionLockUnitTests.AnUnlockedNewestVersion_WhenLocked_ShouldAddOnlyItsDigestAndKeepExistingEntries`; initial lock creation and unchanged input bytes by `SchemaVersionLockIntegrationTests.NoLockFile_WhenTheNewestVersionIsLocked_ShouldCreateItsDigestAndLeaveTheVersionFilesAlone`. B-093's early return is pinned by `SchemaVersionLockUnitTests.AnAlreadyLockedVersion_WhenLockedAgain_ShouldKeepTheLocksBytes`, with unchanged and edited files. The shared digest mechanism is pinned by `AVersionAcrossEveryShippingTree_WhenHashed_ShouldHashOrdinalPathsAndExactBytes`, `AChangedVersionFile_WhenHashed_ShouldChangeTheDigest`, and `AChangedVersionFileSet_WhenHashed_ShouldChangeTheDigest`, using `MockFileSystem` with repository-relative paths. B-089 remains Missing until the separate shipped-set check exists; testing its digest does not claim that check is built.

## 10. Lessons / Spec Deltas

**Independent review correction, 2026-10-14.** ADR-0014 records the owner's
benchmark gate and evaluation of martinothamar/Mediator. The premature CQRS,
model-reader and host-factory migration is removed; schema behavior uses direct
calls through existing services. Process lesson root `0009` records the scope
failure. Product [lesson 0002](lessons/0002-pin-preserves-supported-manifest-encodings.md)
records the encoding defect, and B-045's scenario now covers UTF-8 and both BOM
byte orders of UTF-16 and UTF-32. Existing claim IDs and input semantics remain.

- 2026-10-16, the owner moved `SPEC060` from the approved `spec_status` to the done delivery `status` ([decision 0015](decisions/0015-missing-coverage-is-reported-once-a-feature-is-done.md)). It lands in schema `0.3.0`, because a published package has carried `0.2.0` (OQ-60). B-118 to B-124 added with their scenarios; § 5 row 27 added; OQ-59 and OQ-60 raised. B-117 and `0.2.0` are unchanged.

- 2026-10-15, raised and not answered: OQ-58, what becomes of the rule ids B-115 adds that are not yet built when a release publishes schema `0.2.0`, since each id enters the vocabulary with its own rule (owner, 2026-10-15). No claim, constraint or scenario added; no scenario changed.

- 2026-10-14, the owner decided that a locked, unpublished version may be relocked, and that schema `0.2.0`, never published, is relocked when nine new rules land in it ([decision 0014](decisions/0014-a-locked-unpublished-version-may-be-relocked.md)): `0001-F12`'s six, `0001-F6`'s `SPEC014` and `SPEC024`, and `0101-F9`'s `SPEC015`. C-17 amended in place. OQ-54 resolved; OQ-18 answered in part. Added: B-115 to B-117 and their scenarios. B-093 is unchanged: the relock is a reviewed removal of the entry, then the lock target (B-092). The shipping `0.2.0` files, the feature template's § 9 guidance (B-117) and the version lock change in the delivering pull request. After `spec-reviewer` round 1 (2026-10-15): C-17 cites B-115 for the nine rules and labels the relock mechanism as spec-author's reading; B-117 is decision 0014 call 4, an owner call confirmed 2026-10-15.

- 2026-10-14, the owner stopped shipping schema `0.1.1`, unlocked and never published, so that `0.2.0` is locked and becomes the next published version ([decision 0013](decisions/0013-schema-0-1-1-stops-shipping.md)). Decision 0010 gains a reversal; B-012 holds through `0.2.0`'s explicit pin. OQ-53 resolved in part. No claim text changes: the scenarios of B-053, B-055, B-090, B-092 to B-094 and B-097 to B-100 use version sets without `0.1.1`, and B-102's `0.1.1` example row is removed. § 7 and § 9, which name `0.1.1`, follow in the delivering change.

- 2026-10-14, decision 0012 records recovery on listing failure, validation order, current-pin repair, downgrade preservation and non-interactive exit `2`. B-041 and B-084 amended; B-108 to B-114 and their scenarios added. OQ-14, OQ-17, OQ-20, OQ-23, OQ-48 and OQ-49 resolved. Pin-policy naming and floating remain outside this delivery.

- 2026-10-14, the owner required `specht schema pin` without a version to offer the shipped versions through Spectre.Console's single-choice selection prompt (decision 0005, interactive-selection addendum), replacing the earlier Could classification. B-057 and B-058 now require arrow-key navigation and Enter to select; their scenarios state it, and an additional `@B-046 @boundary` scenario preserves the manifest-only write. The explicit-version CLI remains available. Item `0125` records the required scope; OQ-17, the no-terminal behaviour, remains open. § 9 coverage is unchanged and `Missing`.

- 2026-10-14, the owner named and placed the pin command as `specht schema pin <version>` (decision 0005, command-name addendum). B-043 to B-051 and B-057 name that CLI; the pin scenarios invoke it explicitly. C-13 and § 5 row 11 name it without changing the write boundary. OQ-11 is resolved for the command name and placement; only pin-policy names remain open. The existing interactive pick stays a Could. No production code or test bindings changed; § 9 coverage remains `Missing`.

- 2026-10-13, the owner ratified explicit selection names and CLI precedence, the schema `0.2.0` manifest boundary with a CLI available under any shipped pin, retained upstream verification, and exit `3` with a relative file diagnostic for a missing or invalid selected disk schema (decision 0011). B-009 and B-036 amended; B-101 to B-107 and their scenarios added. § 6 to § 8 record the architecture and proof handoff; § 9 stays `Missing`. No production code, test binding, shipped resource or version lock changes. Manifest OQ-2(b) is answered by this record; its owning row still needs reconciliation.

<!-- last written by: spec-author, 2026-10-16 (decision 0015 recorded) -->

- 2026-10-09, epics made opt-in by the owner ([`0001-F5` decision 0004](../../specht/Manifest/.spec/decisions/0004-epics-are-opt-in-by-the-manifests-epic-grammar.md)), as a change to version 1 that decision 0002 permits before the first publish, as decision 0003 did. A-2 amended. B-037, B-038 and § 5 row 7 added. B-013 and C-1 are untouched: no version has shipped.
- 2026-10-09, the owner replaced the integer schema version with semver (decision 0005). - 2026-10-11, the owner decided how specht's own shipped files are versioned ([decision 0009](decisions/0009-a-versions-folder-is-its-number-one-number-covers-the-set-and-a-version-lock-freezes-it.md)), in three calls. (1) OQ-22: the folder is the version, `v<major.minor.patch>`, in the schema set, the templates and the rule pages, and the `$id` segment is the same, before the `/frontmatter/` `0001-F5` decision 0011 gives it. (2) One number covers the set: templates and rule pages get none of their own, a rule page states the number as `major.minor.patch`, and a change to a template or a rule page alone is a patch and a new version. (3) The newest version is the working version until a committed version lock records it; a test holds each locked version to its digest and allows one unlocked version, the newest; one build target locks and another cuts the next version. Resolved: OQ-22. Amended: C-6; B-012; B-013 with its scenario. Annotated, still open: OQ-21 and OQ-30. Added: need 9; A-4; B-086 to B-100 with their scenarios and § 9 rows, each `Missing`; C-16 and C-17; § 5 rows 22 to 26. The tag check that refuses an unlocked version is `0055-F6` B-013. Not decided by either decision of the day, and held in OQ-52: where one version's manifest sits in the shipping copy; B-086, B-089, B-096, B-100 and C-16 are written on every tree a version's files sit in. Owed to the `implementer`: § 6's row "Which version an embedded folder holds" and § 7's "The folder is not the version", which describe the integer folder as built and name OQ-22 as open. Owed to the `test-writer`: the fifteen new § 9 rows. `spec_status` is `in-review` until § 1-5 are reviewed.

| OQ-39 | Where the fetch sits in the engine, and how a test serves a source. ADR-0010 confines I/O to four stores over the file system and names no network; the testing reference says no test touches the network; the acceptance scenarios run the built tool. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author for the `implementer`, whose ADR it is, and the `test-writer`, who binds "the network is unavailable" and "the source serves". Proposed default: one fetch type behind an interface, registered by the host, recorded in a new ADR beside ADR-0010 and replaced in unit and integration tests; an acceptance scenario serves the source from an address on the test's own machine, and the testing reference is amended to allow that. Not decided: whether an address on the same machine is "the network". | B-025; B-026; B-033; B-067; `0001-F4` B-014 to B-016, B-027 | Resolved 2026-10-12 by the repository owner for the check (B-025): "the network is unavailable" and "no network call was attempted" are proven by two things together. (a) An integration test asserts that the engine and tool assemblies reference no `System.Net` member, extending the metadata guard that holds `0001-F1` B-005 and B-006. (b) An acceptance run of the check, with the process's proxy variables pointed at a closed loopback port, completes. The guard is narrowed when the fetch type is added (`0001-F4` B-014). Rejected: (b) alone; leaving B-025 with no test. The fetch boundary and offline test seam are resolved by implementer, item `0095`: [ADR-0013](../../../.spec/adr/0013-upstream-schema-fetches-use-an-injected-http-client.md) chooses one concrete `UpstreamSchemaFetcher` over an injected `HttpClient`; tests substitute its `HttpMessageHandler`, including in-process command acceptance bindings. No interface or local server is added, and no testing rule permits network access. |

Narrowed 2026-10-13 by the owner (decision 0011): the manifest key first applies at schema `0.2.0`; the CLI option works with any shipped pin. |

The owner's words: "Private sources should be allowed in the first release." and "Assume the private source has no authentication for this version. We can do authenticated sources NEXT version." The first release fetches from a consumer's own source on any `https` host and reads and sends no credential (C-15, amended). The mechanism - keychain, environment, CI - is deferred to the next version and not specified (§ 5 row 20). Decided for then, the owner picking "Recorded host only": a credential is sent only to the host of the recorded `location`, over `https`, and a redirect to another host does not carry it. It is direction for the next version, not a claim of this release; no constraint is written while no credential is sent (decision 0016 call 2). |

| OQ-47 | Which command B-079 names. `init` writes an absent file from the source (`0001-F4` B-014); `upgrade` fetches when the local copy does not match its hash (B-026), and whether an absent file is such a copy is not said. Proposed default: `specht init`, spec-author's reading. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-079 | Resolved 2026-10-12 by the repository owner: `specht init`. Rejected: `specht upgrade`; naming both. B-079 amended to name it. Not asked, and open as OQ-57: an option beside the command. |
| OQ-48 | How does `specht schema list` mark the pinned version? The owner chose that it is marked (OQ-19) and not how. Readings: (a) a prefix on the pinned line, the other lines indented to match, so no line is a version alone; (b) a suffix after the version, so every line starts with its version and an unmarked line is the version alone, as B-041 and B-085 read. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-084; B-041 (the marked line) | Resolved 2026-10-14 (decision 0012): use the suffix ` (pinned)`, as in the reviewed walkthrough; the owner asked that a failing listing provide recovery. |
| OQ-49 | What does `specht schema list` do when the pin cannot be read or is not in the list: `--root` naming a path that is not a directory, a manifest that does not parse or that the check rejects, or a manifest pinning a version the tool does not ship? The owner's answer to OQ-19 covers a root with a manifest and a root without one. B-041 is written with no condition on the root, and whether it holds in these cases is this question. Readings: (a) the plain list and exit `0`, since the list is the tool's; (b) the failure the check gives, exit `2` or `3` (B-018 to B-020, B-003). Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-041; B-084; a claim when answered | Resolved 2026-10-14 (decision 0012): a missing root exits `2`; an unreadable or rejected manifest exits `3`, with no list on stdout and recovery information on stderr (B-108, B-109). |
| OQ-50 | Under a pin policy (B-053, B-054) the check uses a version above the one the manifest pins. Which does `specht schema list` mark: the version pinned, the version used, or both? B-084 marks the one pinned. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-084; B-053; B-054 | Open |
| OQ-51 | `init` now writes the frontmatter schemas and the templates only when asked (`0001-F4` decision 0004), so a root may hold a manifest and neither. Does `upgrade` create a frontmatter schema or a template that is absent, or rewrite only the files present? B-005 rewrites files that "differ" from the pinned version's and does not say whether an absent file differs; B-006 calls a root current when it "already holds what it would write". Readings: (a) `upgrade` rewrites only files present, and a part the consumer never asked for stays absent; (b) `upgrade` writes every file the pinned version ships, which undoes the choice `init` offered; (c) `upgrade` takes the same part options as `init`. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. | B-005; B-006; B-062; item `0045` | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a), with `init --schemas` or `init --templates` the way to add a part. Note, implementer, 2026-10-24: § 7 "Upgrade to the pin" designs item `0045` on (a); (b) or (c) would change only its absent-file row. Item `0093` designs an absent recorded upstream kind on (a) as well, unfetched, with `specht init` the remedy B-079 names. |
| OQ-52 | The names of the lock target and the cut target, how the cut is given its version, where the version lock sits and how it is written, and where the two targets' logic sits so that a test can fail B-092 to B-100: `0055-F1` § 8 holds that the build has no tests, and `0055-F6` proves its tag check by record (`0055-F6` § 5 row 9). Also not decided: what an idle lock run (B-093) or a refused cut (B-094, B-095) says; and where one version's manifest sits in the shipping copy. `0001-F5` decision 0011 moves the live manifest to `.specht/manifest.json`, outside `schema/`, and has the `$id` mirror the file's path under `.specht/` (C-6), and `0001-F4` A-2 and C-2 still say only that the shipping copy is embedded per version from `src/tool/schema/` and `templates/`: whether a version's manifest stays in its schema folder or takes a tree of its own is not written, so B-086, B-089, B-096 and C-16 speak of every tree a version's files sit in and count none. Proposed default, the direction of the plan the owner approved (decision 0009): the lock at `src/tool/schema/versions.lock.json`, outside the folders a version embeds; one SHA-256 for each version over its files in every such tree, in ordinal path order, each file's path and then its bytes; the logic in code a test reaches, a build target only calling it. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author for the `implementer`, who proposes these in § 7, and the `test-writer`. | A-4; B-089; B-092 to B-100; `0055-F6` B-013 | Open |
| OQ-53 | The working version turns out to need another number - cut as `0.1.1`, it gains a change that can fail a tree that passed, which C-11 makes a `0.minor` - or is abandoned. May an unlocked version's folders be renamed or deleted, and by what? B-089 to B-091 bind what is locked and how many versions are not, and nothing else speaks of it. Proposed default: by hand, in a reviewed change - the folders renamed, and each `$id` and rule page row rewritten to the new number - and no target is built for it. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-086; B-087; B-096 | Resolved in part 2026-10-14 by the repository owner (decision 0013): schema `0.1.1`, unlocked and never published, is abandoned - its folders deleted by hand in a reviewed change, with no target built for it. Renaming an unlocked version is not decided and stays open. |
| OQ-54 | A version locked and never published - the lock merged, and a fault found before its tag. May its lock entry be removed so the version can be edited, or is a locked version changed only by a new version, as a published one is (C-1)? C-17 and B-093 are written on the second reading. Proposed default: the second - once the entry is on `main` the version is treated as published and the fix is the next version, at the cost of a number no package carries. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | C-17; B-089; B-093 | Resolved 2026-10-14 by the owner (decision 0014): a locked version no published package has carried may be relocked; a published one changes only by a new version (C-1). The relock, a reviewed removal of the entry and then the lock target (B-092), is spec-author's reading, not stated by the owner, as in C-17; so B-093 is unchanged. C-17 amended. |
| OQ-55 | A published `0.x.y` a later tool stops shipping (C-1): does its lock entry stay? And what fails when a locked version `1.0.0` or higher has no folder, which B-004 forbids? B-089 binds a locked version the tool ships, and says nothing of one it does not. Proposed default: an entry is never removed; a locked version below `1.0.0` with no folder is passed over, and one `1.0.0` or higher with no folder fails the same check, which gives B-004 its mechanism. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-004; B-089; C-17 | Open |
| OQ-56 | This repository's live copy after a cut. `0001-F4` B-004 holds `.specht/manifest.json`, `.specht/schema/frontmatter/` and `.specht/templates/` to the newest embedded version, and the cut writes only the new version's folders (B-100), so that comparison fails from the cut until the live copy and this repository's pin move to the new version. Who moves them: the cut, the maintainer with the pin command and `specht upgrade` (B-043, B-005; neither is built), or a hand edit? Proposed default: the maintainer, in the same change as the cut, by hand until those commands exist; the cut does not write `.specht/`. Raised 2026-10-11 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-100; `0001-F4` B-004 | Open |
| OQ-57 | Does the message of B-079 name an option beside `specht init`? Since `0001-F4` decision 0004 (2026-10-11) a run's selection holds the frontmatter schemas only with `--schemas` or `--all` (`0001-F4` A-1), and `init` fetches only when they are in the selection (`0001-F4` B-014), so `specht init` alone, beside the manifest the check read, fetches no file. The owner chose `specht init` over `specht upgrade` (OQ-47) and was not asked about an option. Readings: (a) the message names `specht init --schemas`; (b) it names `specht init` alone. Proposed default: (a); under (b) a consumer who runs what the message names gets the same failure again. B-079 as amended holds under both. Raised 2026-10-12 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-079 (the option) | Open |
| OQ-58 | Each id B-115 adds enters schema `0.2.0`'s vocabulary in the pull request that builds its rule (owner, 2026-10-15), so B-014 holds at every commit, and `0.2.0` is relocked as each rule lands (C-17; decision 0014). Decision 0013 makes `0.2.0` the next published version. A release cut before all nine rules are built publishes `0.2.0` with only the ids then in it, and C-1 and B-015 freeze it there: decision 0014 allows no relock once a package has carried it, so the ids still to come cannot enter `0.2.0` and B-115 cannot hold. What becomes of them? Options: (a) no release carries `0.2.0` until B-115 holds - all nine ids in its vocabulary, each with its rule; (b) the release goes, and the ids not yet built move to a `0.3.0`, which needs the owner to amend B-115 and decision 0014's calls 2 and 3, and spends a number decision 0014 rejected. Under (a) the wait may be long: `SPEC015`'s specification (`0101-F9`) is a draft, and `SPEC014` and `SPEC024` (`0001-F6`) have no item. Recommendation: (a), because it changes no claim and no decision. Whether a check enforces it, such as the release guard (`0055-F6` B-013) refusing a tag while `0.2.0`'s vocabulary is short of B-115's, is a claim the answer would add. Raised 2026-10-15 by spec-author; reworded the same day for the owner's per-rule ruling. Owner not asked. | B-115; C-1; C-17 | Open |
| OQ-59 | Should `SPEC060` report a § 9 `Missing` row once the item delivering that claim is done, rather than once the whole Feature is done? The consumer item schema carries no claim ids, so the rule cannot find that item. Proposed default: not until items carry claims; B-118 keys on the Feature's delivery status. Raised 2026-10-16 by spec-author on the owner's ruling that it is not built now. | § 5 row 27 | Open |
| OQ-60 | Is schema `0.2.0` published? The release run for tag `v0.1.131` (2026-10-15) pushed the `specht.tool` package `0.1.131`, which embeds `0.2.0` at lock digest `5d087a05…`; `main` locks `0.2.0` at `3e86b5…`, relocked with the matrix-resolution manifest keys (item `0222`) on a commit after the tagged one, so the package and `main` hold two different `0.2.0`s. Under C-1, C-17 and decision 0014 call 1 a carried version is never relocked, so (a) the change of decision 0015 is `0.3.0`, and `main`'s relocked `0.2.0` is a published version changed in place, for the owner to rule on with OQ-58; or (b) a pre-release package does not publish a schema version, `0.2.0` may still be relocked, and B-118 to B-124 read `0.2.0` for `0.3.0`. Proposed default: (a), because C-1 says "published in a package" and names no exception for a pre-release. Raised 2026-10-16 by spec-author. Owner not asked. | B-118 to B-124; C-1; C-17; OQ-58 | Open |
| OQ-61 | Is a frontmatter schema or template that differs from the shipped file only in line endings current? Compared byte-exact, a checkout with CRLF line endings (`core.autocrlf`) never counts as current, and `upgrade` rewrites it on every run. Readings: (a) byte-exact, as § 7 designs it; (b) line endings normalised before comparing; (c) byte-exact, with the rewrite keeping the on-disk file's line endings. Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-005; B-006 | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a), the identity the version lock uses (C-17). |
| OQ-62 | What does `upgrade` do when a write fails after earlier files were rewritten? No claim states the exit code, or whether the files already rewritten stay rewritten. Readings: (a) exit `3` naming the root-relative file, as the pin command does, with earlier files left rewritten and a second run completing the rest, as § 7 designs it; (b) the files already rewritten are restored. Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-005; B-007; item `0046` | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a). |
| OQ-63 | Does "created" in B-007 and B-063 cover a file that exists only during one write? The atomic replace creates a staging sibling under `.specht/templates/` or `.specht/schema/frontmatter/` and always deletes it. Readings: (a) the claims bind the end state, and the staging sibling is outside them; (b) they bind every moment, and the replace stages elsewhere. Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-007; B-063; C-2 | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a); the claims stand as written. |
| OQ-64 | B-006 counts every manifest key the pinned version defines among what makes a root current, and adding a missing key is item `0047`'s (B-016), so until `0047` lands `0045` judges files only. Readings: (a) `0045`'s tests for B-006 use manifests that already hold every key, and `0047` extends the judgement to a missing key; (b) B-006 is split, its manifest-key part a claim of its own that `0047` carries. Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-006; items `0045`, `0047` | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a). |
| OQ-65 | The `templates` member of `spechtSchema` and its key form - the template's file name under `.specht/templates/`, such as `feature.md` - are spec-author's reading of the owner's "one SHA-256 hash per fetched template beside the schema hashes" (decision 0016 call 5); the owner named no member, and renamed decision 0008's key at its ratification. Readings: (a) `templates`, keyed by file name, beside `hashes`; (b) entries inside `hashes` keyed by file name, beside the kinds; (c) another name for (a). Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-125; B-126; B-127 | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a), which B-125 to B-127 are written on. |
| OQ-66 | What is a `spechtSchema` holding `templates` under a pin below `0.3.0`? B-128 and B-129 read it as changing nothing, as today's tool ignores a member it does not know, so no published version's verdict changes (C-1). Readings: (a) ignored, as B-128 and B-129 state; (b) rejected as a member that version does not know, as B-102 rejects `schemaSource` and OQ-46 proposes for an unknown member, which changes `0.1.0`'s verdict. That the member is `0.3.0`'s at all rests on OQ-60 (a); under OQ-60 (b) it could be `0.2.0`'s. Raised 2026-10-24 by spec-author. | B-125; B-128; B-129; OQ-46; OQ-60 | Open. Owner not asked. Proposed default: (a). |
